; the specific choice does not matter, as its sole purpose is to let us examine the set of

Z
-values without needing to know which value is associated with which data point. Define also
Z_{(p)}=\left(Z_{(p(1))},\ldots,Z_{(p(n+m))}\right)
for each
p\in\mathcal{S}_{n+m}
, and let
P\in\mathcal{S}_{n+m}
be the permutation given by the ranks of the true observed vector
Z
, so that
Z=Z_{(P)}
. Under
g\propto r\,f
, we can use Bayes theorem to show that the distribution of the true ranks
P
, conditional on the order statistics
Z_{()}
, is given by

\mathbb{P}\left\{P=p\mid Z_{()}\right\}=\frac{\prod_{i\in\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}}r(Z_{(p(i))})}{\sum_{\tilde{p}\in\mathcal{S}_{n+m}}\prod_{i\in\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}}r(Z_{(\tilde{p}(i))})}.

(17)

As a result, if
P^{(1)},\ldots,P^{(H)}
are drawn independently from (
17
), under
H_{0}
the vectors
Z,\,Z_{(P^{(1)})},\ldots,Z_{(P^{(H)})}
are conditionally independent and identically distributed given
Z_{()}
. Consequently, after marginalising over
Z_{()}
, the sequence
\left(Z,Z_{(P^{(1)})},\ldots,Z_{(P^{(H)})}\right)
is exchangeable.

We now conclude the proof by showing that (
3
) and (
17
) are equivalent. For fixed
Z
, and thus
P
, the map
\sigma\mapsto p:=\sigma\circ P
is a bijection over
\mathcal{S}_{n+m}
. This, together with
Z_{\sigma(i)}=Z_{(\sigma\circ P(i))}=Z_{(p(i))}
, allows showing that the weight
\prod_{i=n+1}^{n+m}r(Z_{\sigma(i)})
in (
3
) can be rewritten as
\prod_{i=n+1}^{n+m}r(Z_{(p(i))})
, which corresponds to the weight in (
17
). Therefore, under the change of variables
p=\sigma\circ P
, the distribution of
\sigma^{(h)}
given
Z
in (
3
) induces the distribution of
P^{(h)}=\sigma^{(h)}\circ P
given
Z_{()}
in (
17
), and conversely. In particular,

(\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(H)})
and
(P^{(1)},\ldots,P^{(H)})
have corresponding laws via this bijection, and since
Z_{\sigma^{(h)}}=Z_{(\sigma^{(h)}\circ P)}=Z_{(P^{(h)})}
for each
h\in[H]
, the joint law of
(Z,Z_{\sigma^{(1)}},\ldots,Z_{\sigma^{(H)}})
is the same as that of

(Z,Z_{(P^{(1)})},\ldots,Z_{(P^{(H)})})
. As we have already shown that the latter is exchangeable, this
implies the finite-sample validity of (
2
) for every test statistic
T
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
2
.

The proof consists of simply checking the detailed balance equations for the Markov chain defined by the algorithm.
Denote with
\mathcal{P}
the collection of all
K
couples of indices
\{(i_{1},j_{1}),\ldots,(i_{K},j_{K})\}
such that
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{K})
contains distinct elements from
[n]
, and
(j_{1},\ldots,j_{K})
contains distinct elements from
\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}
. For any
\tau\in\mathcal{P}
and any permutations
p,p^{\prime}
, we write
p\sim_{\tau}p^{\prime}
if
p
can be transformed to
p^{\prime}
by swapping any subset of the pairs in
\tau
. We now compute the transition probability matrix of the Markov chain defined by Algorithm
1
. Every probability sign has to be intended conditionally on
Z
. For all
t\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and any permutations
p,p^{\prime}
, we have

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p\right\}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}|}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p,\tau_{t}=\tau\right\},

since at each time
t
, Step 3 of the algorithm corresponds to drawing
\tau_{t}\in\mathcal{P}
uniformly at random. Next, given
\tau_{t}=\tau:=\{(i_{1},j_{1}),\ldots,(i_{K},j_{K})\}
and
\sigma_{t-1}=p
, it must be the case that
\sigma_{t}
satisfies
\sigma_{t}\sim_{\tau}p
by definition of Steps 4-5 of the algorithm. In light of the definition of the odds ratio for each
B_{i_{k},j_{k}}^{t}
in (
4
), we see that for any
p^{\prime},p^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\tau}p
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p,\tau_{t}=\tau\right\}}{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p,\tau_{t}=\tau\right\}}=\prod_{j\in\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{K}\}}\frac{r(Z_{p^{\prime}(j)})}{r(Z_{p^{\prime\prime}(j)})}=\prod_{j\in\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{K}\}}\frac{r(Z_{p^{\prime}(j)})}{r(Z_{p^{\prime\prime}(j)})}\prod_{j\notin\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{K}\}}\frac{r(Z_{p^{\prime}(j)})}{r(Z_{p^{\prime\prime}(j)})}

\displaystyle=\prod_{j\in\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}}\frac{r(Z_{p^{\prime}(j)})}{r(Z_{p^{\prime\prime}(j)})}=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime}\right\}}{\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime\prime}\right\}},

(18)

where in the second equality we used the fact that
r(Z_{p^{\prime}(j)})/r(Z_{p^{\prime\prime}(j)})=1
for all
j\notin\{j_{1},\ldots,j_{K}\}
, while in the last step we used the definition of the distribution (
3
). Therefore,

\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p\right\}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}|}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{P}}\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{p^{\prime}\sim_{\tau}p\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime}\right\}}{\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{p^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\tau}p\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime\prime}\right\}}.

This, together with the fact that
\sim_{\tau}
defines an equivalence relation on
\mathcal{P}
, shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\{\sigma=p\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p^{\prime}\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p\right\}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}|}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{P}\{\sigma=p\}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{p^{\prime}\sim_{\tau}p\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime}\right\}}{\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{p^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\tau}p\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime\prime}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{P}|}\sum_{\tau\in\mathcal{P}}\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime}\right\}\cdot\frac{\mathbbm{1}\left\{p\sim_{\tau}p^{\prime}\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\{\sigma=p\}}{\sum_{p^{\prime\prime}}\mathbbm{1}\left\{p^{\prime\prime}\sim_{\tau}p^{\prime}\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime\prime}\right\}}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma=p^{\prime}\right\}\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\sigma_{t}=p\mid\sigma_{t-1}=p^{\prime}\right\}.

(19)

This verifies the detailed balance equations, and so the Markov chain is reversible and has (
3
) as stationary distribution. Moreover, since
r(x)
is assumed to be positive for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, it follows that the chain is aperiodic and irreducible, ensuring the uniqueness of the stationary distribution.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The proof relies fundamentally on the reversibility of the Markov chain defined in Algorithm
1
, a property established in the proof of Proposition
2
. This reversibility permits an alternative but distributionally equivalent sampling procedure whenever the input
\sigma
is drawn from the stationary distribution: we may first sample
\sigma_{*}
from distribution (
3
) conditional on
Z
, and subsequently generate
(\sigma,\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(H)})
through
H+1
independent applications of Algorithm
1
, each running for
S
steps and initialized at
\sigma_{*}
. The independence of these runs, combined with their shared initialization point
\sigma_{*}
, ensures that
(\sigma,\sigma^{(1)},\ldots,\sigma^{(H)})
are independently and identically distributed when conditioned on
\sigma_{*}
and
Z
. This conditional independence directly implies the exchangeability of
\left(Z,Z^{(1)},\ldots,Z^{(H)}\right)
since, under
H_{0}
, the distribution of
Z_{\sigma_{0}}\mid Z_{()}
, with
\sigma_{0}=\mathrm{id}_{n+m}
, coincides with the distribution of
Z_{\sigma}\mid Z_{()}
, where
\sigma
is sampled from (
3
). This concludes the proof.
∎

A.2
Proofs for Section
3

Proof of Lemma
4
.

We begin by proving that there exists a unique
\lambda_{0}
such that
h=\frac{nf+mg}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}
is a density function. Define the function

\displaystyle F:\begin{cases}\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}\\
\lambda\mapsto\int\frac{nf+mg}{n+\lambda mr}d\mu\end{cases}

(20)

which can easily be seen to be continuous. It also straightforward to see that
F
is strictly decreasing, since if
\lambda_{2}>\lambda_{1}>0
we have

\frac{nf(x)+mg(x)}{n+\lambda_{2}mr(x)}\leq\frac{nf(x)+mg(x)}{n+\lambda_{1}mr(x)}\quad\text{ for all }x\in\mathcal{X},

and this inequality is strict in the support of
f
and
g
as we are assuming that
r(x)>0
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
. It is clear that
\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow 0^{+}}F(\lambda)=1+m/n>1
, while
\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}F(\lambda)=0<1
. Thus, by the intermediate value theorem and the strict monotonicity of
F
we have established our first claim that
\lambda_{0}
exists and is unique.

It can now be seen that
h
satisfies the requirements of the result. Indeed, we have

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle=\int hd\mu=\int\frac{nf+mg}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}d\mu=\frac{1}{n}\int\frac{n}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}(nf+mg)d\mu

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\int\left(1-\frac{\lambda_{0}mr}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\right)(nf+mg)d\mu=\frac{1}{n}\left(\int(nf+mg)d\mu-\int(\lambda_{0}mr)\frac{nf+mg}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}d\mu\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left(n+m-\lambda_{0}m\int rhd\mu\right)=1+\frac{m}{n}-\lambda_{0}\frac{m}{n}\int rhd\mu,

which implies that
\lambda_{0}=(\int rhd\mu)^{-1}
. This shows us that
nh+m(\int rhd\mu)^{-1}rh=nh+\lambda_{0}mrh=nf+mg
, so that
h
gives rise to null distributions for which the distribution of the combined sample matches that of our data, concluding the existence part of the result.

As for its uniqueness, observe that any density
h
preserving the combined distribution under the null hypothesis must be of the form
h
for some
\lambda_{0}
positive, since

\displaystyle nf+mg

\displaystyle=nh+m\frac{rh}{\int rfd\mu}=\left\{n+mr\left(\int rhd\mu\right)^{-1}\right\}h=\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr\right)h.

But the
\lambda_{0}
such that
h
integrates to
1
is unique. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Suppose that the null hypothesis holds, so that
g=rf/\int rfd\mu
. It follows from Lemma
4
and its proof that
f=h
and that
\lambda_{0}=(\int rfd\mu)^{-1}
. We therefore see that
\lambda_{0}rf-g=0
, so indeed
T_{\mathcal{F},r}(f,g)=0
.

We now turn to the reverse implication. Assuming that
f
and
g
are such that
T_{\mathcal{F},r}(f,g)=0
, we will show that
H_{0}
must be true. By assumption,
\mathcal{H}
is dense in
C^{0}_{b}(\mathcal{X})
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
, so that for all
\phi\in C^{0}_{b}(\mathcal{X})
and all
\epsilon>0
there exists
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\|\varphi-\phi\|_{\infty}\leq\epsilon
.
Thus

\displaystyle\frac{n+m}{m}

\displaystyle\bigg|\int\frac{\lambda_{0}rf-g}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}\phi\,d\mu\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n+m}{m}\left|\int\frac{\lambda_{0}rf}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}(\phi-\varphi)d\mu\right|+\frac{n+m}{m}\left|\int\frac{\lambda_{0}rf-g}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}\varphi d\mu\right|+\frac{n+m}{m}\left|\int\frac{g}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}(\phi-\varphi)d\mu\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\,\lambda_{0}rf\,|\phi-\varphi|d\mu+\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\,T_{\mathcal{F},r}(f,g)+\int\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\,g\,|\phi-\varphi|d\mu

\displaystyle=\int\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\,\lambda_{0}rf\,|\phi-\varphi|d\mu+\int\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\,g\,|\phi-\varphi|d\mu

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\,\int\frac{\lambda_{0}r}{\min(1,\lambda_{0}r)}\,fd\mu+\epsilon\,\int\frac{1}{\min(1,\lambda_{0}r)}\,gd\mu=\epsilon\left(\mathbb{E}[\max\{1,\lambda_{0}r(X)\}]+\mathbb{E}[\max\{1,1/\lambda_{0}r(Y)\}]\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon\,\left(2+\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{0}r(X)]+\mathbb{E}[1/\lambda_{0}r(Y)]\right)=\epsilon\,\left(2+\lambda_{0}\int rf\,d\mu+\lambda_{0}^{-1}\int g/r\,d\mu\right),

where in the first equality we used our assumption that
T_{\mathcal{F},r}(f,g)=0
. Because
\epsilon>0
was arbitrary, and since
\int rf\,d\mu<\infty
,
\int g/r\,d\mu<\infty
by assumption, and
\lambda_{0}<\infty
by Proposition
17
in Appendix
B
, we now see that
\frac{n+m}{m}\int\frac{\lambda_{0}rf-g}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}\phi\,d\mu=0
for all
\phi\in C^{0}_{b}(\mathcal{X})
, which further implies

\frac{mg}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}=\frac{\lambda_{0}mrf}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\text{ a.s.}

by Lemma 9.3.2 in
Dudley (2002)
. Simplifying this inequality we see that
g=\lambda_{0}rf\propto rf
, as required.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6
.

We assume throughout the proof that
H_{0}
does not hold and show that
\mathbb{P}\{p>\alpha\}\rightarrow 0
as
n,m\rightarrow\infty
. Recall our notation
\bar{R}
and
\check{R}
for the arithmetic and harmonic means of the
r
values, respectively, and write
A
for the event that
\bar{R}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})
and
1/\check{R}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(1/\check{R})
. On this event, we have

\bar{R}/\check{R}\leq 4\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\leq 4\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\{r(Z)^{2}\}\mathbb{E}\{r(Z)^{-2}\}}\leq 4\sqrt{V_{0}},

which will allow us to apply Lemma
7
. Since we assume that
H>\lceil 1/\alpha-1\rceil
we have
\alpha(1+H)-1>0
, and we can therefore use Markov’s inequality to see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\{p>\alpha\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{1+\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{T(Z_{\sigma^{(h)}})\geq T(Z)\}>\alpha(1+H)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{T(Z_{\sigma^{(h)}})\geq T(Z)\}\right]}{\alpha(1+H)-1}=\frac{H}{\alpha(1+H)-1}\mathbb{P}\{T(Z_{\sigma^{(1)}})\geq T(Z)\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{H}{\alpha(1+H)-1}\bigl[\mathbb{P}\{T(Z_{\sigma^{(1)}})\geq T(Z),A\}+\mathbb{P}(A^{\complement})\bigr],

where the penultimate step follows from exchangeability. We begin by controlling
\mathbb{P}(A^{\complement})
by combining Chebyshev’s inequality with the fact that
\max(\mathbb{E}[\left\{\lambda_{0}r(Z)\right\}^{2}],\,\mathbb{E}[\left\{\lambda_{0}r(Z)\right\}^{-2}])\leq V_{0}
. In particular, this follows from
\mathbb{E}[\left\{r(Z)\right\}^{2}]\,\mathbb{E}[\left\{r(Z)\right\}^{-2}]\leq V_{0}
and

\frac{1}{\mathbb{E}(1/\check{R})}=\frac{n+m}{n\int f/r\,d\mu+m\int g/r\,d\mu}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{n+m}\biggl(n\int rf\,d\mu+m\int rgd\mu\biggr)=\mathbb{E}(\bar{R}),

where the latter holds by Jensen’s inequality. Together, these allow showing

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(A^{\complement})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{\bar{R}}{\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})}>2\biggr)+\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{1}{\check{R}\,\mathbb{E}(1/\check{R})}>2\biggr)\leq\frac{\mathrm{Var}(\bar{R})}{\mathbb{E}^{2}(\bar{R})}+\frac{\mathrm{Var}(1/\check{R})}{\mathbb{E}^{2}(1/\check{R})}\leq\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\lambda_{0}\bar{R}\bigr)+\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}\check{R}}\biggr)\leq\frac{2V_{0}}{m+n}.

(21)

Using the shorthand
\sigma=\sigma^{(1)}
it now suffices to consider
T(Z_{\sigma})
and
T(Z)
on the event
A
.

We will first prove that
\mathbb{E}[|T(Z_{\sigma})|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]=\mathbb{E}[T(Z_{\sigma})\mathbbm{1}_{A}]\rightarrow 0
. Write

W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}:=\frac{1}{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}\varphi(Z_{\sigma(i)})-\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{n}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(j)})}\varphi(Z_{\sigma(j)})\right),

so that we have
T(Z_{\sigma})=\frac{n+m}{m}\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|
. In particular,
W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}
coincides with the term inside the conditional expectation in the statement of Lemma
7
. Write
N\equiv N(\delta)\equiv N\left(\{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\},\delta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)
for the covering number of
\{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
and let
\{\psi_{1},\ldots,\psi_{N}\}
be an associated
\delta
-cover. Now, for a generic function
\varphi_{0}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
\|\varphi_{0}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|\,\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}(|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|-|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|)\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]+\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\varphi_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\\
\|\varphi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}(|W_{\varphi_{1}}^{\sigma}|-|W_{\varphi_{2}}^{\sigma}|)\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]+\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|\varphi_{1}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\\
\|\varphi_{2}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left|W_{\varphi_{1}}^{\sigma}-W_{\varphi_{2}}^{\sigma}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]+\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}\|_{\infty}\leq\delta}\left|W_{\varphi_{1}}^{\sigma}-W_{\varphi_{2}}^{\sigma}\right|\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{i\in[N]}\left|W_{\psi_{1}}^{\sigma}-W_{\psi_{i}}^{\sigma}\right|\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]+\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq\delta}|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|\right]+(4N+1)\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}],

(22)

where the penultimate inequality follows from a one-step discretization argument as in
Wainwright (2019, Equation 5.34)
and the final inequality follows on bounding the maximum by a sum, using the fact that
W_{\varphi_{1}}^{\sigma}-W_{\varphi_{2}}^{\sigma}=W_{\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2}}^{\sigma}
for any
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}
, and using the triangle inequality to say that
\max_{i\in[N]}\|\psi_{1}-\psi_{i}\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 2
. We will now bound each term on the right-hand side of (
22
) separately. As for the first, observe that by (
8
) we have

\displaystyle|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}\varphi(Z_{\sigma(i)})-\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{n}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(j)})}\varphi(Z_{\sigma(j)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}+\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{1}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(j)})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\min\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(i)})},\,\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{n}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{\sigma(j)})}\biggr\}\leq\frac{2\min(m,n)}{n}\|\varphi\|_{\infty}

for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}
, which implies that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\infty}\leq\delta}|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|\right]\leq\frac{2\min(m,n)}{n}\delta.

We now turn to the second term in (
22
). With our assumption that
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma\|\cdot\|_{\mathcal{H}}
for a constant
\gamma
, it follows from the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, (
25
) and Lemma
7
that for any
\varphi
such that
\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1
we have

\mathbb{E}[|W_{\varphi_{0}}^{\sigma}|\mathbbm{1}_{A}]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[(W_{\varphi}^{\sigma})^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{A}\right]}\leq\gamma\biggl\{\frac{16\,V_{0}^{1/4}\min(m^{1/2},n^{1/2})}{n}+\exp\biggl(-\frac{S}{64\tau V_{0}^{1/2}}\biggr)\biggr\}.

Putting these bounds together and using
T(Z_{\sigma})=\frac{n+m}{m}\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}|W_{\varphi}^{\sigma}|
, we see that for any
\delta>0
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle[T(Z_{\sigma})\mathbbm{1}_{A}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\min(m,n)(n+m)}{nm}\delta+(4N(\delta)+1)\,\gamma\,\biggl\{\frac{16\,V_{0}^{1/4}\sqrt{\min(n,m)}(n+m)}{nm}+\frac{n+m}{m}\exp\biggl(-\frac{S}{64\tau V_{0}^{1/2}}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq 8\delta+(4N(\delta)+1)\,\gamma\,\biggl\{\frac{32\,V_{0}^{1/4}}{\sqrt{\min(n,m)}}+\frac{n+m}{m}\exp\biggl(-\frac{S}{64\tau V_{0}^{1/2}}\biggr)\biggr\}.

(23)

Since we assume that
N\left(\{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1\},\delta,\|\cdot\|_{\infty}\right)
is finite for all
\delta>0
, and that
S\gg\tau\log(m+n)
, there exists a sequence
(\delta_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}}
such that this right-hand side converges to zero.

We now turn to the unpermuted statistic
T(Z)
. Recall the population quantity

T_{\mathcal{F},r}\equiv T_{\mathcal{F},r}(f,g)=\,\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}|I_{\varphi}|,\quad\text{ where }\quad I_{\varphi}:=\frac{n+m}{m}\int\frac{\lambda_{0}rf-g}{n/m+\lambda_{0}r}\varphi\,d\mu

with
\lambda_{0}
such that
\int\frac{nf+mg}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}d\mu=1
. As we are working under the alternative hypothesis, we know by Lemma
5
and Proposition
17
in Appendix
B
that
T_{\mathcal{F},r}>0
and we may fix
\varphi
such that
\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1
and
I_{\varphi}\geq T_{\mathcal{F},r}/2
. Then we have

\displaystyle T(Z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{n+m}{nm}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(X_{i})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(X_{i})}\varphi(X_{i})-\frac{n+m}{nm}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{n}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Y_{j})}\varphi(Y_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathcal{F},r}+\frac{n+m}{m}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr(X_{i})\varphi(X_{i})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(X_{i})}-\int\frac{\lambda_{0}mr}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi f-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{m\varphi(Y_{j})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Y_{j})}+\int\frac{m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi g\biggr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathcal{F},r}-\gamma\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|\,\frac{n+m}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\lambda_{0}mr(Z_{i})}{(n+\lambda_{0}mr(Z_{i}))(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{i}))}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{n+m}{m}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})\varphi(X_{i})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})}-\int\frac{\lambda_{0}mr}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi f-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{m\varphi(Y_{j})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y_{j})}+\int\frac{m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi g\biggr|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathcal{F},r}-\gamma\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|\,\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\max\{1,\lambda_{0}r(Z_{i})\}}{\min\{1,\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{i})\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{n+m}{m}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})\varphi(X_{i})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})}-\int\frac{\lambda_{0}mr}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi f-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{m\varphi(Y_{j})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y_{j})}+\int\frac{m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi g\biggr|

\displaystyle=:\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathcal{F},r}-\gamma\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|\,\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\max\{1,\lambda_{0}r(Z_{i})\}}{\min\{1,\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{i})\}}-R_{1}.

The final term is a sum of independent mean-zero random variables, so that we may use Chebyshev’s inequality to control the probability of this event. Indeed, using again the boundedness assumption on the second moments of
r
we can see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle(R_{1}\geq\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\biggl(\frac{n+m}{m}\biggl|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})\varphi(X_{i})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{i})}-\int\frac{\lambda_{0}mr}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi f-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{m\varphi(Y_{j})}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y_{j})}+\int\frac{m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi g\biggr|\geq\epsilon\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}m^{2}}\left\{n^{-1}\operatorname{Var}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}mr(X)\varphi(X)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X)}\right)+m^{-1}\operatorname{Var}\left(\frac{m\varphi(Y)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y)}\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}(n+m)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}m^{2}}\biggl\{n^{-1}\min\biggl(\frac{m^{2}}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{0}^{2}r^{2}(X)],\,1\biggr)+\frac{m}{n^{2}}\min\biggl(1,\frac{n^{2}}{m^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\lambda_{0}^{-2}r^{-2}(Y)]\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}(n+m)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}m^{2}}\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\min\biggl(\frac{m^{2}(m+n)}{n^{3}}V_{0},\,1\biggr)+\frac{m}{n^{2}}\min\biggl(1,\frac{n^{2}(m+n)}{m^{3}}V_{0}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma^{2}(n+m)^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}m^{2}}V_{0}\biggl\{\mathbbm{1}_{\{n\leq m\}}\biggl(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{m+n}{m^{2}}\biggr)+\mathbbm{1}_{\{n>m\}}\biggl(\frac{m^{2}(m+n)}{n^{4}}+\frac{m}{n^{2}}\biggr)\biggr\}\leq\frac{12\gamma^{2}V_{0}}{\epsilon^{2}\,\min(n,m)}.

(24)

It remains for us to control the term depending on
\widehat{\lambda}/\lambda_{0}
under the event
A
. We first note that our assumption on
r
gives us bounds on
\widehat{\lambda}
and
\lambda_{0}
. Exactly as in the proof of Lemma
7
, on the event
A
we have that
1/\{2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\}\leq\widehat{\lambda}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})
. As also claimed above, by a very similar argument we also have that
1/\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\leq\lambda_{0}\leq\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})
. We may view
\widehat{\lambda}
as an
M
-estimator of
\lambda_{0}
. Indeed, writing
\phi_{\lambda}(z)=(n+m)/\{n+m\lambda r(z)\}-1
, we defined
\widehat{\lambda}
to be the solution to

\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda):=\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\phi_{\lambda}(Z_{i})=0.

Similarly,
\lambda_{0}
can be thought of as the solution to
S_{m,n}(\lambda):=\mathbb{E}\{\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda)\}=0
. By Jensen’s inequality we have that

\displaystyle-\widehat{S}_{m,n}^{\prime}(\lambda)=\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{mr(Z_{i})}{\{n+\lambda mr(Z_{i})\}^{2}}\geq\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\{n+\lambda mr(Z_{i})\}^{2}}{mr(Z_{i})}}=\frac{m(n+m)}{n^{2}\check{R}^{-1}+2\lambda mn+\lambda^{2}m^{2}\bar{R}},

which is decreasing in
\lambda
. Thus, for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle(|\widehat{\lambda}/\lambda_{0}-1|\geq\epsilon,A)=\mathbb{P}(|\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0}|\geq\epsilon\lambda_{0},A)\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\widehat{\lambda})-\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|\geq\epsilon\lambda_{0}\bigl|\widehat{S}_{m,n}^{\prime}\bigl((1+\epsilon)\lambda_{0}\bigr)\bigr|,A\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|\geq\epsilon\lambda_{0}\bigl|\widehat{S}_{m,n}^{\prime}\bigl(2\lambda_{0}\bigr)\bigr|,A\biggr)\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|\geq\frac{(m+n)m\lambda_{0}\epsilon}{2\{n^{2}\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})+2\lambda_{0}mn+\lambda_{0}^{2}m^{2}\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|\geq\frac{(m+n)m\epsilon}{2}\min\biggl[\frac{1}{n^{2}\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})+2mn+m^{2}},\,\frac{1}{n^{2}+2mn+m^{2}\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})}\biggr]\biggr)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\biggl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|\geq\frac{m\epsilon}{2V_{0}^{1/2}(m+n)}\biggr),

where the last step follows from the boundedness assumption. Now
\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})
is a sum of independent mean-zero random variables, so that we may again use Chebyshev’s inequality to control the probability of this event. Indeed, it is straightforward to see that for
\delta=m\epsilon/\{2V_{0}^{1/2}(m+n)\}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(|\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})|

\displaystyle\geq\delta\bigr)\leq\delta^{-2}\mathrm{Var}\bigl(\widehat{S}_{m,n}(\lambda_{0})\bigr)=\frac{1}{\delta^{2}(m+n)^{2}}\bigl\{n\mathrm{Var}\,\phi_{\lambda_{0}}(X)+m\mathrm{Var}\,\phi_{\lambda_{0}}(Y)\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{4V_{0}}{\epsilon^{2}}\biggl\{n\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{1-\lambda_{0}r(X)}{n+m\lambda_{0}r(X)}\biggr)+m\mathrm{Var}\biggl(\frac{1-\lambda_{0}r(Y)}{n+m\lambda_{0}r(Y)}\biggr)\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4V_{0}}{\epsilon^{2}(m+n)^{2}}\biggl[n\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\{1-\lambda_{0}r(X)\}^{2}}{\min^{2}(1,\lambda_{0}r(X))}\biggr\}+m\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\frac{\{1-\lambda_{0}r(Y)\}^{2}}{\min^{2}(1,\lambda_{0}r(Y))}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4V_{0}}{\epsilon^{2}(m+n)^{2}}\biggl[n\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\bigl(\lambda_{0}r(X)\bigr)^{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}r(X)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}+m\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\bigl(\lambda_{0}r(Y)\bigr)^{2}+\biggl(\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}r(Y)}\biggr)^{2}\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8V_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}(m+n)}.

Finally, since on the event
A
we have
\bar{R}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})
,
\check{R}^{-1}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})
,
1/\{2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\}\leq\widehat{\lambda}\leq 2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})
,
1/\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\leq\lambda_{0}\leq\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})
and
\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\leq V_{0}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\max\{1,\lambda_{0}r(Z_{i})\}}{\min\{1,\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{i})\}}\leq\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\left(1+\lambda_{0}r(Z_{i})\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{i})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\left(1+\lambda_{0}r(Z_{i})+\frac{1}{\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{i})}+\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\widehat{\lambda}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{n+m}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\left(1+\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})r(Z_{i})+\frac{2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})}{r(Z_{i})}+2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\right)

\displaystyle=1+\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\,\bar{R}+2\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\,\check{R}^{-1}+2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\leq 1+2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\,\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})+4\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\,\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})+2\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})

\displaystyle\leq 1+8\mathbb{E}(\check{R}^{-1})\mathbb{E}(\bar{R})\leq 1+8V_{0}^{1/2}.

This, in conjunction with (
24
) and the previous display, gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle(T(Z)<\tfrac{1}{4}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{2}T_{\mathcal{F},r}-\gamma(1+8V_{0}^{1/2})\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|\,-R_{1}<\tfrac{1}{4}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A\right)\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma(1+8V_{0}^{1/2})\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|\,+R_{1}>\tfrac{1}{4}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\gamma(1+8V_{0}^{1/2})\,\biggl|\frac{\widehat{\lambda}}{\lambda_{0}}-1\biggr|>\tfrac{1}{8}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(R_{1}>\tfrac{1}{8}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A\right)\leq\frac{2^{9}V_{0}^{2}\gamma^{2}(1+8V_{0}^{1/2})^{2}}{T_{\mathcal{F},r}^{2}(n+m)}+\frac{2^{10}\gamma^{2}V_{0}}{T_{\mathcal{F},r}^{2}\min(n,m)}.

We can thus bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(T(Z_{\sigma})\geq T(Z),A)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}(T(Z_{\sigma})\geq\tfrac{1}{4}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A)+\mathbb{P}(T(Z)<\tfrac{1}{4}T_{\mathcal{F},r},A)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\mathbb{E}[T(Z_{\sigma})\mathbbm{1}_{A}]}{T_{\mathcal{F},r}}+\frac{2^{9}V_{0}^{2}\gamma^{2}(1+8V_{0}^{1/2})^{2}}{T_{\mathcal{F},r}^{2}(n+m)}+\frac{2^{10}\gamma^{2}V_{0}}{T_{\mathcal{F},r}^{2}\min(n,m)},

and use (
23
) to show that the last display goes to zero. This, combined with (
21
), concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7
.

Let
(\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1},\ldots,\sigma_{S})
be the permutations generated through Algorithm
1
. For
t\in\{0,1,\ldots,S\}
define

S_{n}^{t}:=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)})-n\int\varphi d\widehat{H}_{n,m},

Introduce the shorthand
r^{t}_{i}:=r(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)})
and
\varphi_{i}^{t}:=\varphi(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)})
for
i\in[n+m]
and define
q_{i}^{t}:=\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}/(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t})
. Then we see that

\displaystyle\frac{S_{n}^{t}}{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}^{t}-\int\varphi d\widehat{H}_{n,m}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}^{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\varphi(Z_{i})}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{i})}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varphi_{i}^{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{\varphi_{i}^{t}}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}}\varphi_{i}^{t}-\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\frac{n}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{j}^{t}}\varphi_{j}^{t}\biggr)=\frac{1}{n}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\varphi_{i}^{t}-\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}(1-q_{j}^{t})\varphi_{j}^{t}\biggr\}.

(25)

Recall that we write
K=\min(m,n)
and, for each
t\geq 0
, we generate
\sigma_{t+1}
by sampling
I_{1},\ldots,I_{K}\in[n]
and
J_{1},\ldots,J_{K}\in\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}
uniformly at random without replacement, and switching
Z_{\sigma_{t}(I_{\ell})}
with
Z_{\sigma_{t}(J_{\ell})}
with probability

\tilde{p}_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}^{t}=\frac{\widehat{\lambda}nmr_{I_{\ell}}^{t}}{(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{I_{\ell}}^{t})(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{J_{\ell}}^{t})}=q_{I_{\ell}}^{t}(1-q_{J_{\ell}}^{t}).

Given
(I_{\ell}),(J_{\ell})
, for
\ell\in[K]
let
B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}\sim\mathrm{Ber}(\tilde{p}_{i_{\ell},j_{\ell}}^{t})
determine whether the
\ell
th switch occurs.

Having introduced the necessary notation, we prove the result by analysing the change in
S_{n}^{t}
over a single step of the algorithm, showing that
S_{n}^{t}
can be regarded as a Lyapunov function satisfying a geometric drift condition. We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{(S_{n}^{t+1})^{2}|\sigma_{t},Z\}

\displaystyle-(S_{n}^{t})^{2}=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{S_{n}^{t}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\biggr\}^{2}\biggm|\sigma_{t},Z\biggr]-(S_{n}^{t})^{2}

\displaystyle=2S_{n}^{t}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\biggm|\sigma_{t},Z\biggr\}+\mathbb{E}\biggl[\biggl\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\biggr\}^{2}\biggm|\sigma_{t},Z\biggr].

(26)

We treat each of the two terms in this sum separately. For the first, we may calculate that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\biggl\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\biggm|\sigma_{t},Z\biggr\}=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\tilde{p}_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}^{t}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\bigm|\sigma_{t},Z\bigr\}=K\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\tilde{p}_{I_{1},J_{1}}^{t}(\varphi_{J_{1}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{1}}^{t})\bigm|\sigma_{t},Z\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{mn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(\varphi_{j}^{t}-\varphi_{i}^{t})=\frac{K}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)\biggl\{\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}(1-q_{j}^{t})\varphi_{j}^{t}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\varphi_{i}^{t}\biggr\}=-\frac{K}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)S_{n}^{t},

(27)

where the penultimate equality follows from (
8
). The second term in (
26
) is controlled by writing

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\biggl[\biggl\{\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t})\biggr\}^{2}\biggm|\sigma_{t},Z\biggr]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{B_{I_{\ell},J_{\ell}}(\varphi_{I_{\ell}}^{t}-\varphi_{J_{\ell}}^{t})^{2}\bigm|\sigma_{t},Z\bigr\}+\sum_{\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}}\mathbb{E}\bigl\{B_{I_{\ell_{1}},J_{\ell_{1}}}B_{I_{\ell_{2}},J_{\ell_{2}}}(\varphi_{I_{\ell_{1}}}^{t}-\varphi_{J_{\ell_{1}}}^{t})(\varphi_{I_{\ell_{2}}}^{t}-\varphi_{J_{\ell_{2}}}^{t})\bigm|\sigma_{t},Z\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{mn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{ij}^{t}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})^{2}+\frac{K(K-1)}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\tilde{p}_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{t}\tilde{p}_{i_{2}j_{2}}^{t}(\varphi_{i_{1}}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{1}}^{t})(\varphi_{i_{2}}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{2}}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{mn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{ij}^{t}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})^{2}+\frac{K(K-1)}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}\biggl[\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{i_{1}j_{1}}^{t}\tilde{p}_{i_{2}j_{2}}^{t}(\varphi_{i_{1}}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{1}}^{t})(\varphi_{i_{2}}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{2}}^{t})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\sum_{i_{1},i_{2}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{i_{1}j}^{t}\tilde{p}_{i_{2}j}^{t}(\varphi_{i_{1}}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})(\varphi_{i_{2}}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})-\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j_{1},j_{2}=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{ij_{1}}^{t}\tilde{p}_{ij_{2}}^{t}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{1}}^{t})(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j_{2}}^{t})

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}(\tilde{p}_{ij}^{t})^{2}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle=\frac{K}{mn}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}\tilde{p}_{ij}^{t}\biggl\{1+\frac{K-1}{(m-1)(n-1)}\tilde{p}_{ij}^{t}\biggr\}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})^{2}+\frac{K(K-1)}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}\biggl[\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}(S_{n}^{t})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}(1-q_{j}^{t})^{2}\biggl\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})\biggr\}^{2}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(q_{i}^{t})^{2}\biggl\{\sum_{j=n+1}^{n+m}(1-q_{j}^{t})(\varphi_{i}^{t}-\varphi_{j}^{t})\biggr\}^{2}\biggr]

\displaystyle\leq\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}\biggl\{\frac{8K\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{mn}+\frac{K(K-1)}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}(S_{n}^{t})^{2}\biggr\},

(28)

where for the final inequality we again use (
8
). Combining (
26
), (
27
) and (
28
), using our assumption that
\max(m,n)\geq 3
and using the fact that by (
8
) we have
\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\leq K
, we see that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\{

\displaystyle(S_{n}^{t+1})^{2}|\sigma_{t},Z\}-(S_{n}^{t})^{2}\leq-\frac{2K}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)(S_{n}^{t})^{2}+\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}\biggl\{\frac{8K\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{mn}+\frac{K(K-1)}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}(S_{n}^{t})^{2}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=-\frac{2K}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)\biggl\{1-\frac{K-1}{2(m-1)(n-1)}\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr\}(S_{n}^{t})^{2}+\frac{8K\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{K}{2mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)(S_{n}^{t})^{2}+\frac{8K\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}.

We now control
\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}
using our boundedness assumption on
r
. Before doing so we establish some basic consequences of this assumption. First, we see that it implies bounds on
\widehat{\lambda}
. Indeed, writing
\bar{R}=(n+m)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}r(Z_{i})
for the arithmetic mean of the observed
r
values, by Jensen’s inequality we have that

1=\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{1}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{i})}\geq\biggl[\frac{1}{(n+m)^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr(Z_{i})\}\biggr]^{-1}=\biggl(\frac{n}{n+m}+\frac{m}{n+m}\widehat{\lambda}\bar{R}\biggr)^{-1}.

Similarly, writing
\check{R}=\{(n+m)^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}r(Z_{i})^{-1}\}^{-1}
for the harmonic mean of the observed
r
values, we have that

1=\sum_{i=1}^{n+m}\frac{1}{m+\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}nr(Z_{i})^{-1}}\geq\biggl(\frac{m}{n+m}+\frac{n}{m+n}\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}\check{R}^{-1}\biggr)^{-1},

where the first identity follows by the symmetry of our problem, as discussed in Remark
2
. These two bounds together imply that
\bar{R}^{-1}\leq\widehat{\lambda}\leq\check{R}^{-1}
. Together with our assumption that
\bar{R}/\check{R}\leq\kappa
, this implies that
\max(\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}\check{R}^{-1},\widehat{\lambda}\bar{R})\leq(\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}\check{R}^{-1})\cdot(\widehat{\lambda}\bar{R})=\bar{R}/\check{R}\leq\kappa
. Having bounds on
\widehat{\lambda}
, we now turn to the control of
\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}
itself. Let
(\cdot)
be a reordering of our data such that
r(Z_{(1)})\leq r(Z_{(2)})\leq\ldots\leq r(Z_{(m+n)})
. Then, again using Jensen’s inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\geq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{m\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{(i)})}{n+m\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{(i)})}

\displaystyle\geq n\biggl\{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{n+m\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{(i)})}{m\widehat{\lambda}r(Z_{(i)})}\biggr\}^{-1}=\frac{mn}{m+\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}r(Z_{(i)})^{-1}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{mn}{m+(n+m)\widehat{\lambda}^{-1}\check{R}^{-1}}\geq\frac{mn}{m+(n+m)\kappa}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{mn}{(m+n)\kappa}.

Having bounded
\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}
we may now conclude the proof. Writing
\tau=\max(m,n)/\min(m,n)
and arguing inductively for the third inequality, we now see that

\displaystyle n^{-2}\mathbb{E}\{(S_{n}^{t+1})^{2}|Z\}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-2}\mathbb{E}\{(S_{n}^{t})^{2}|Z\}+n^{-2}\mathbb{E}\biggl\{-\frac{K}{2mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)(S_{n}^{t})^{2}+\frac{8K\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}}{mn}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}^{t}\biggr)^{2}\biggm|Z\biggr\}

\displaystyle\leq n^{-2}\biggl\{1-\frac{K}{4(m+n)\kappa}\biggr\}\mathbb{E}\{(S_{n}^{t})^{2}|Z\}+\frac{8\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}K^{3}}{mn^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}K^{3}}{mn^{3}}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\biggl(1-\frac{1}{8\tau\kappa}\biggr)^{s}+\biggl(1-\frac{1}{8\tau\kappa}\biggr)^{t+1}n^{-2}\mathbb{E}\{(S_{n}^{0})^{2}|Z\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{64\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}K^{3}}{mn^{3}}\tau\kappa+\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}\exp\biggl(-\frac{t+1}{8\tau\kappa}\biggr)\leq\frac{64\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}\kappa K}{n^{2}}+\|\varphi\|_{\infty}^{2}\exp\biggl(-\frac{t+1}{8\tau\kappa}\biggr),

and the result follows upon taking
t=S-1
.

∎

A.3
Proofs for Section
4

Proof of Proposition
8
.

This proof borrows ideas from the proof of Lemma 4 in
Gretton et al. (2012)
. Define the linear operator

T_{rf}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}\\
\varphi\mapsto\int\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\,\lambda_{0}rf\,\varphi\,d\mu.\end{cases}

Write
d\mu_{x}=d\mu(x)
. Using the reproducing property of the reproducing kernel Hilbert space, i.e.
\varphi(x)=\langle\varphi,k(\cdot,x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
, we can show that this operator is bounded, since for all
\varphi\in\mathcal{H}
we have

\displaystyle|T_{rf}\varphi|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(x)f(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}|\varphi(x)|d\mu_{x}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(x)f(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}\left|\langle\varphi,k(\cdot,x)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}\right|d\mu_{x}

\displaystyle\leq\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}\sqrt{k(x,x)}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(x)f(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}d\mu_{x},

which shows that
|T_{rf}\varphi|/\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}
is bounded uniformly in
\varphi
. The same is true for the linear operator

T_{g}:\begin{cases}\mathcal{H}\to\mathbb{R}\\
\varphi\mapsto\int\frac{(n+m)\,g}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi\,d\mu,\end{cases}

hence the Riesz representation theorem implies that there exist
m_{rf},m_{g}\in\mathcal{H}
such that
T_{rf}\varphi=\langle m_{rf},\varphi\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
and
T_{g}\varphi=\langle m_{g},\varphi\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}
. Furthermore, using again the reproducing property of
\mathcal{H}
, we have that

\displaystyle m_{rf}(t)

\displaystyle=\langle m_{rf},k(t,\cdot)\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}=T_{rf}k(t,\cdot)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(x)f(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}k(x,t)d\mu_{x}

and, similarly,
m_{g}(t)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)g(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}k(x,t)d\mu_{x}
. This implies that

\displaystyle T_{\mathcal{F},r}^{2}(f,g)

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}\left|\int\frac{(n+m)(\lambda_{0}rf-g)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}\varphi d\mu\right|\right)^{2}=\left(\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}\left|T_{rf}\varphi-T_{g}\varphi\right|\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sup_{\|\varphi\|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq 1}|\langle m_{rf}-m_{g},\varphi\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}|\right)^{2}=\|m_{rf}-m_{g}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\langle m_{rf},m_{rf}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}+\langle m_{g},m_{g}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}-2\langle m_{rf},m_{g}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(t)f(t)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(t)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(x)f(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}k(x,t)d\mu_{x}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)g(t)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(t)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)g(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}k(x,t)d\mu_{x}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-2\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)\lambda_{0}r(t)f(t)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(t)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{X}}\frac{(n+m)g(x)}{n+\lambda_{0}mr(x)}k(x,t)d\mu_{x}\right)d\mu_{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X,X^{\prime}}\left[\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}(n+m)^{2}r(X)r(X^{\prime})}{\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X)\}\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X^{\prime})\}}k(X,X^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\mathbb{E}_{Y,Y^{\prime}}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y)\}\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y^{\prime})\}}k(Y,Y^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt-2\mathbb{E}_{X,Y}\left[\frac{\lambda_{0}(n+m)^{2}r(X)}{\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(X)\}\{n+\lambda_{0}mr(Y)\}}k(X,Y)\right],

where
X,X^{\prime}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}f
independently of
Y,Y^{\prime}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}g
, and concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9
.

Consider a kernel function
K:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
that satisfies the assumptions in Subsection
4.2
. In particular,
K\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R})\cap L^{4}(\mathbb{R})
implies that
K\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
using the basic inequality
t^{2}\leq t+t^{4}
for
t\geq 0
. Based on this, we define the constants

\kappa_{j}(d):=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|K(u)|^{j}\,du\right)^{d}<\infty\quad\text{ for }j\in\{1,2,4\},

which are finite by assumption. Since the kernel is fixed in advance, we omit its dependence from the notation. We frequently use the analytical properties of the product kernel
k_{\zeta}
with bandwidth
\zeta\geq 1
, which satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|k_{\zeta}(x,y)|^{j}\,dy=\zeta^{(j-1)d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}}|K(u)|^{j}\,du\right)^{d}=\kappa_{j}(d)\,\zeta^{(j-1)d}\quad\text{ for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\text{ and }j\in\{1,2,4\}.

As a result, using the fact that
\max(\|f\|_{\infty},\|g\|_{\infty})\leq M
, it follows that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|k_{\zeta}(x,X)|^{j}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|k_{\zeta}(x,y)|^{j}\,f(y)dy\leq M\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|k_{\zeta}(x,y)|^{j}\,dy=M\kappa_{j}(d)\zeta^{(j-1)d}\quad\text{ for }j\in\{1,2,4\},

and similarly for the expectation of
|k_{\zeta}(x,Y)|^{j}
. Furthermore, these bounds imply the same bounds on the quantities
\mathbb{E}[|k_{\zeta}(X_{1},X_{2})|^{j}],\,\mathbb{E}[|k_{\zeta}(Y_{1},Y_{2})|^{j}]
and
\mathbb{E}[|k_{\zeta}(X,Y)|^{j}]
for all
j\in\{1,2,4\}
. Now, we already know that Algorithm
2
controls the type I error at a nominal level
\alpha
, so we can bound the minimax separation
\rho_{r}^{*}
by controlling its type II error. In order to do this, fix
\beta\in(0,1-\alpha)
, choose
H\geq 2\lceil\frac{1}{\alpha\beta}-1\rceil
, and suppose
(f,g)\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{r}(\rho)
satisfies

\displaystyle\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)\geq\max\left\{2|\mathbb{E}[U(Z)-U(Z_{\sigma})]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|,\left(\frac{8}{\alpha\beta}\operatorname{Var}[U(Z_{\sigma})-U(Z)]\right)^{1/2}\right\}.

(29)

Then, a double application of Markov’s inequality shows that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{p>\alpha\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(1+\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\left\{U(Z_{\sigma^{(h)}})\geq U(Z)\right\}>(1+H)\alpha\right)\leq\frac{1+H\,\mathbb{P}\left\{U(Z_{\sigma})\geq U(Z)\right\}}{(1+H)\alpha}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1+H)\alpha}\left(1+\frac{H\,\operatorname{Var}[U(Z_{\sigma})-U(Z)]}{\left\{\mathbb{E}[U(Z_{\sigma})-U(Z)]\right\}^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{(1+H)\alpha}\left(1+\frac{H\alpha\beta}{2}\right)\leq\beta.

Bounding the terms on the right-hand side of (
29
) is therefore sufficient to establish an upper bound on the minimax separation with respect to the squared shifted maximum mean discrepancy metric. However, since our goal is to characterize the separation in terms of the
L^{2}
distance defined in (
13
), we must also relate
\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}
to this
L^{2}
norm. The proof proceeds in two main steps: first, we analyze the expectation and variance terms appearing on the right-hand side of (
29
) and derive appropriate upper bounds. In the second step, we express the squared shifted maximum mean discrepancy as the sum of the square of the separation metric in (
13
) and the bias term
\|\psi_{r}-\varphi_{\zeta}\ast\psi_{r}\|_{2}^{2}
, where we define
\varphi_{\zeta}(u)\;:=\;\zeta^{d}\,\prod_{i=1}^{d}K\!\bigl(\zeta\,u_{i}\bigr)
for
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, so that
k_{\zeta}(x,y)=\varphi_{\zeta}(x-y)
for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then control the bias term using the smoothness assumptions associated with the Sobolev class. Combining these two steps yields an upper bound on
\rho^{*}_{r}
. Throughout the following we set
\zeta=n^{\frac{2}{4s+d}}
so that in particular
n^{-2}\zeta^{d}=n^{-\frac{8s}{4s+d}}\leq 1
. Also, for parameters
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
, we denote by
Q(a_{1},\ldots,a_{k})
a constant that depends only on these parameters. Its value may change from line to line, but it may only depend on
a_{1},\ldots,a_{k}
.

\bullet
Mean and variance of
U(Z_{\sigma})

We begin by analyzing the second moment of the permuted statistic
U(Z_{\sigma})
. Throughout this bullet point, we rescale the kernel so that
K(0)=1
; this normalization is inessential and serves only to simplify notation, and the bound in (
36
) remains valid after absorbing the resulting scaling factor into
Q_{0}
. Furthermore, sums over
i
’s are to be intended for
i
varying in
[n]
, sums over
j
’s are to be intended for
j
varying in
\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}
, and sums over
k
’s are to be intended for
k
varying in
[n+m]
. The strategy is to relate the distribution of
U_{\sigma}:=U(Z_{\sigma})
,
where
\sigma
is sampled from
(
3
)
, to the distribution of
U_{\sigma_{t}}\equiv U_{t}
, where
\sigma_{t}
is the permutation at time
t\in\mathbb{N}
of an equivalent version of Algorithm
1
that is initialized at stationarity. More precisely, we consider a procedure that at each time step
t
samples
i\in[n]
and
j\in\{n+1,\ldots,n+m\}
uniformly at random, and switches
Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)}
with
Z_{\sigma_{t}(j)}
with probability

\tilde{p}_{i,j}^{t}:=\mathbb{P}\{\text{switch }i\text{ and }j\text{ at time }t\mid i,j\text{ are selected}\}=\frac{\widehat{\lambda}nmr_{i}^{t}}{(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t})(n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{j}^{t})},

where
r^{t}_{i}:=r(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)})
for all
i\in[n+m]
. As already outlined in Remark
1
, this algorithm still targets the distribution (
3
) since
\tilde{p}_{i,j}^{t}/\tilde{p}^{t}_{j,i}=r_{i}^{t}/r_{j}^{t}
. Write

\displaystyle K_{ij}^{t}

\displaystyle:=k_{\zeta}(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)},Z_{\sigma_{t}(j)})\text{ and }K_{i}^{t}:=k_{\zeta}(Z_{\sigma_{t}(i)},\cdot),

\displaystyle q_{i}^{t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}}{n+\widehat{\lambda}mr_{i}^{t}}\text{ and }S_{n}^{t}:=\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}=\sum_{j}(1-q_{j}^{t}),

where the last equality holds by (
8
). In particular we have
\tilde{p}_{i,j}^{t}=q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})
. Observe that under the assumption that
0<c\leq r(x)\leq C<\infty
for all
x\in\mathcal{X}
, we have that
C^{-1}\leq\widehat{\lambda}\leq c^{-1}
, which implies
mc\,(mc+nC)^{-1}\leq q_{i}^{t}\leq mC\,(mC+nc)^{-1}
and
S_{n}^{t}\geq nmc\,(nC+mc)^{-1}
. We define the V-statistic

V_{t}=\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\left\|\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}-\sum_{j}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t}\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=:\|G_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\langle G_{t},G_{t}\rangle_{\mathcal{H}},

which coincides with (
11
) evaluated on our data at time
t
with kernel
k_{\zeta}
, and will be convenient for our analysis. We further define the U-statistic

\displaystyle U_{t}

\displaystyle=V_{t}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i}(q_{i}^{t})^{2}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{j}(1-q_{j}^{t})^{2}

\displaystyle=V_{t}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i}(q_{i}^{t})^{2}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{j}\{1+(q_{j}^{t})^{2}-2q_{j}^{t}\}

\displaystyle=V_{t}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{k}(q_{k}^{t})^{2}+\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{j}(1-q_{j}^{t})

\displaystyle=V_{t}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}S_{n}^{t}\underbrace{-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{k}(q_{k}^{t})^{2}+\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m}}_{\text{permutation independent}},

which has the same distribution as
U_{\sigma}
for each
t\in\mathbb{N}
. If we swap
i
and
j
at time
t
, then

\displaystyle U_{t+1}-U_{t}

\displaystyle=\left\|G_{t}+\frac{n+m}{nm}(K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t})\right\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\|G_{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}).

By stationarity, we therefore have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=nm\,\mathbb{E}[U^{2}_{t+1}-U^{2}_{t}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\left(\left\{U_{t}+\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\right.\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\left.\hskip 250.0pt-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})\right\}^{2}-U^{2}_{t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\cdot 2U_{t}\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})\right\}^{2}\right],

(30)

which is the sum of a linear and a quadratic term. In order to simplify this right-hand side we now provide some useful identities. We notice that

\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t})=S_{n}^{t}\left\{\sum_{j}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t}-\sum_{i}q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}\right\}=-\frac{nm}{n+m}S_{n}^{t}G_{t},

\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}=\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(2\zeta^{d}-2K_{ij}^{t})=2\zeta^{d}\,(S_{n}^{t})^{2}-2\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t},

and

\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})=S_{n}^{t}\left\{\sum_{j}q_{j}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})-\sum_{i}(q_{i}^{t})^{2}\right\}=S_{n}^{t}\left\{m-S_{n}^{t}-\sum_{k}(q_{k}^{t})^{2}\right\}.

Using these identities, the linear term in (
30
) gives

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\cdot 2U_{t}\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[2U_{t}\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},\sum_{i,j}(K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t})q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}\,q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 250.0pt-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[2U_{t}\left\{-2S_{n}^{t}\left(U_{t}+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}S_{n}^{t}+\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{k}(q_{k}^{t})^{2}-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\quad\quad\quad+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(S_{n}^{t})^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}S_{n}^{t}\left(m-S_{n}^{t}-\sum_{k}(q_{k}^{t})^{2}\right)\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=-4\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}U^{2}_{t}\right]-4\mathbb{E}\left[U_{t}\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}\right].

(31)

As for the quadratic term, it is useful to define
G_{t}^{-(i,j)}:=G_{t}-(n+m)n^{-1}m^{-1}\{q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}-(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t}\}
and write

\displaystyle\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}-\frac{n+m}{nm}\{q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}-(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t}\},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\langle q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}-(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\|K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\|_{\mathcal{H}}^{2}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\langle q_{i}^{t}K_{i}^{t}-(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{j}^{t},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(\zeta^{d}-K^{t}_{ij})-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{i}^{t}K_{ij}^{t}-\zeta^{d}q_{i}^{t}-\zeta^{d}(1-q_{j}^{t})+(1-q_{j}^{t})K^{t}_{ij})

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(\zeta^{d}-K^{t}_{ij})-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{j}^{t}-q_{i}^{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})K^{t}_{ij}.

(32)

Combining (
30
), (
31
), (
32
) gives

\displaystyle 4

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}U^{2}_{t}\right]=-4\mathbb{E}\left[U_{t}\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})K^{t}_{ij}\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\mathbb{E}\left[|U_{t}|\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})|K_{ij}^{t}|\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 50.0pt+\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\left\{\frac{2(n+m)}{nm}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle+\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})K^{t}_{ij}\right\}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{4}m^{4}}\Big\{\sum_{i,j}q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})|K_{ij}^{t}|\Big\}^{2}\right]}+\frac{8(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{8(n+m)^{4}}{n^{4}m^{4}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})^{2}(K^{t}_{ij})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\sum_{i,j}\{q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}\}^{2}\right]}+\frac{8(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\frac{8(n+m)^{4}}{n^{4}m^{4}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})^{2}(K^{t}_{ij})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 12\max\left\{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\sum_{i,j}\{q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}\}^{2}\right]}\,,\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right],\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.\frac{2(n+m)^{4}}{n^{4}m^{4}}\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})^{2}(K^{t}_{ij})^{2}\right]\right\}.

(33)

We will now bound each of the three quantities inside the maximum separately. If the first quantity reaches the maximum, we can use
m\leq n\leq\tau m
,
q_{k}^{t}\in[0,1]
and
S_{n}^{t}\geq\frac{nmc}{nC+mc}
to show that

\displaystyle\frac{nmc}{nC+mc}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\left[U^{2}_{t}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}U^{2}_{t}\right]\leq 3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\sum_{i,j}\{q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})K_{ij}^{t}\}^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\sum_{i,j}(K_{ij}^{t})^{2}\right]}\leq 3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\sum_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}(K_{k_{1},k_{2}}^{t})^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\left\{\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})+\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}k_{\zeta}^{2}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})+2\sum_{i,j}k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{i},Y_{j})\right\}\right]}

\displaystyle=3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\left\{n(n-1)k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{1},X_{2})+m(m-1)k_{\zeta}^{2}(Y_{1},Y_{2})+2nmk_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{1})\right\}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\,\frac{(n+m)^{6}}{n^{3}m^{3}}\,M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}}\leq 3\sqrt{(1+\tau)^{6}\,\tau^{-3}\,\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\,M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}}.

(34)

Note that in the fourth inequality we bound
\sum_{i,j}(K^{t}_{i,j})^{2}
by
\sum_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}(K^{t}_{k_{1},k_{2}})^{2}
, the latter being more advantageous to analyze due to its permutation invariance. Making (
34
) explicit for the expectation of
U_{t}^{2}
shows that there exists a constant
Q_{0}\equiv Q_{0}(c,C,d,\tau,M)
such that
\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\leq Q_{0}\frac{\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}}
, in the case where the first quantity in the maximum in (
33
) dominates. As for the case when the third quantity dominates, we can argue exactly as in (
34
) to show that

\displaystyle\frac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{4}m^{4}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})(q_{i}^{t}-q^{t}_{j})^{2}(K^{t}_{ij})^{2}\right]\leq\frac{4(n+m)^{6}}{n^{4}m^{4}}\,M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d},

(35)

which implies that
\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\leq Q_{0}\frac{\zeta^{d}}{n^{3}}
. Finally, as for the term involving
G_{t}^{-(i,j)}
, we have

\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{i}^{t}(1-q_{j}^{t})\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}-K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]\leq 2\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]+2\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{j}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right],

and we can analyze the evolution of the right hand side through a version of Algorithm
1
where indices
i
and
j
remain fixed. In other words, the Algorithm works the same, but we are just allowed to swap indices
\tilde{i}\neq i
with
\tilde{j}\neq j
with probability
q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q^{t}_{\tilde{j}})
. We show in Lemma
15
that this procedure still preserves the stationary distribution. The two terms above can be bounded by almost identical arguments so we will restrict attention to the first. In analysing the evolution of
\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}
, it is useful to write

\displaystyle\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}

\displaystyle q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})(K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}-K_{\tilde{i}}^{t})=\left(\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}\right)\left(\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}\right)-\left(\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})\right)\left(\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{nm}{n+m}S_{n}^{t}G_{t}^{-(i,j)}+(1-q_{j}^{t})\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}.

Under stationarity, we thus have

\displaystyle 0=(n-1)(m-1)\,\mathbb{E}[\langle G_{t+1}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}-\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})\left\{\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)}+\tfrac{n+m}{nm}(K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}-K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}),K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}-\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\,q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})\langle K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}-K_{\tilde{i}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]+\tfrac{2(n+m)}{nm}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\langle\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})(K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}-K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}),K_{i}^{t}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\,q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})\langle K_{\tilde{j}}^{t}-K_{\tilde{i}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{2(n+m)}{nm}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\langle(1-q_{j}^{t})\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{2(n+m)^{2}}{nm^{2}}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]+\tfrac{2(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}m}\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle K_{\tilde{i}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]-2\mathbb{E}[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{2(n+m)}{nm}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\langle(1-q_{j}^{t})\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}\max_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathbb{E}[k^{2}_{\zeta}(Z_{k_{1}},Z_{k_{2}})]-2\mathbb{E}[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tfrac{2(n+m)}{nm}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\langle(1-q_{j}^{t})\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}-2\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\tfrac{2(n+m)}{nm}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\langle(1-q_{j}^{t})\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t}-q_{i}^{t}\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}-2\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2\sqrt{2}\tfrac{n+m}{nm}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\langle\sum_{\tilde{i}\neq i}q_{\tilde{i}}^{t}K_{\tilde{i}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}+\langle\sum_{\tilde{j}\neq j}(1-q_{\tilde{j}}^{t})K_{\tilde{j}}^{t},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}-2\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]+2\sqrt{2}\tfrac{(n+m)^{2}}{nm}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]\max_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathbb{E}[k^{2}_{\zeta}(Z_{k_{1}},Z_{k_{2}})]}

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}-2\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]+2\sqrt{2}\tfrac{(n+m)^{2}}{nm}\sqrt{M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]}.

Using again the fact that
S_{n}^{t}\geq\frac{nmc}{nC+mc}
, we can employ the previous calculations to show that

\displaystyle\frac{2nmc}{nC+mc}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}\left[S_{n}^{t}\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tfrac{(n+m)^{4}}{n^{2}m^{2}}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}+2\sqrt{2}\tfrac{(n+m)^{2}}{nm}\sqrt{M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq 2(\tau+1)^{4}\tau^{-2}\,\max\left\{M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d},\,\sqrt{M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}\mathbb{E}\left[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}\right]}\right\}.

In particular, this yields
\mathbb{E}[\langle G_{t}^{-(i,j)},K_{i}^{t}\rangle^{2}]\leq Q_{0}\frac{\zeta^{d}}{n}
, which further implies that
\mathbb{E}[U_{t}^{2}]\leq Q_{0}\frac{\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}}
also when the second quantity in (
33
) attains the maximum. Combining this with (
34
) and (
35
) gives

\displaystyle\max\left\{\mathbb{E}^{2}[|U_{\sigma}|],\operatorname{Var}[U_{\sigma}]\right\}\leq\mathbb{E}[U^{2}_{\sigma}]\leq Q_{0}\frac{\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}},

(36)

and concludes the analysis for
U_{\sigma}
.

\bullet
Mean and variance of
U_{\mathrm{id}}

In what follows, we define
U_{\mathrm{id}}:=\,U(Z)
, which
targets
\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)
, and we study its
mean and variance. This would be straightforward if we used the normalization factors
\{n(n-1)\}^{-1}
and
\{m(m-1)\}^{-1}
instead of
n^{-2}
and
m^{-2}
, and if
\widehat{\lambda}
were not random. The former problem is easy to address. Define

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}=\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n(n-1)m^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}r(X_{i_{1}})r(X_{i_{2}})k_{\zeta}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})}{\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i_{1}})\}\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i_{2}})\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{m^{3}(m-1)}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}=1}^{m}\frac{k_{\zeta}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})}{\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(Y_{j_{1}})\}\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(Y_{j_{2}})\}}-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{nm^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i})k_{\zeta}(X_{i},Y_{j})}{\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i})\}\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(Y_{j})\}},

(37)

and observe that
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}-U_{\mathrm{id}}
equals

\displaystyle\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n^{2}(n-1)m^{2}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}=1}^{n}\frac{\widehat{\lambda}^{2}r(X_{i_{1}})r(X_{i_{2}})k_{\zeta}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})}{\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i_{1}})\}\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(X_{i_{2}})\}}+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{m^{4}(m-1)}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}=1}^{m}\frac{k_{\zeta}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})}{\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(Y_{j_{1}})\}\{\frac{n}{m}+\widehat{\lambda}r(Y_{j_{2}})\}}.

Due to the boundedness assumption on
r
, this implies that there exists a constant
Q_{1}\equiv Q_{1}(c,C,\tau)>0
such that

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[U_{\mathrm{id}}]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|+\mathbb{E}[|U_{\mathrm{id}}-\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}|]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|+\frac{Q_{1}}{n^{3}}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}=1}^{n}|k_{\zeta}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})|+\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}=1}^{m}|k_{\zeta}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|+\frac{Q_{1}M\kappa_{1}(d)}{n},

(38)

and

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}[U_{\mathrm{id}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\operatorname{Var}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]+2\operatorname{Var}[U_{\mathrm{id}}-\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\operatorname{Var}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]+\frac{Q_{1}}{n^{2}}\left(\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\mathbb{E}[k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})]+\max_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\mathbb{E}[k_{\zeta}^{2}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})]\right)\leq 2\operatorname{Var}[\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}]+\frac{Q_{1}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}}{n^{2}}.

(39)

Since
\zeta\geq 1
, we have
1/n\leq\zeta^{d/2}/n=\sqrt{\zeta^{d}/n^{2}}
, and therefore the additional mean term in (
38
) is of no larger order than the second-moment scale given in (
36
), namely
\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[U_{\sigma}^{2}]}\lesssim\sqrt{\zeta^{d}/n^{2}}
. Similarly, the additional variance term in (
39
) matches the order in (
36
). Thus, it suffices to control the moments of
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}
.

\bullet
Mean of
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}

We now address the harder problem of having
\widehat{\lambda}
in
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}
, instead of the non-random quantity
\lambda_{0}
appearing in the definition of
\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)
. To overcome this issue, for
\lambda>0
define

\displaystyle G(\lambda):=

\displaystyle\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{n(n-1)m^{2}}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}=1}^{n}\frac{\lambda^{2}r(X_{i_{1}})r(X_{i_{2}})k_{\zeta}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}})}{\{n/m+\lambda r(X_{i_{1}})\}\{n/m+\lambda r(X_{i_{2}})\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{(n+m)^{2}}{m^{3}(m-1)}\sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}=1}^{m}\frac{k_{\zeta}(Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})}{\{n/m+\lambda r(Y_{j_{1}})\}\{n/m+\lambda r(Y_{j_{2}})\}}

\displaystyle-\frac{2(n+m)^{2}}{nm^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\frac{\lambda r(X_{i})k_{\zeta}(X_{i},Y_{j})}{\{n/m+\lambda r(X_{i})\}\{n/m+\lambda r(Y_{j})\}},

(40)

so that
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}=G(\widehat{\lambda})
. By expanding
G
around
\lambda_{0}
using a Taylor sum up to the second order we get
G(\widehat{\lambda})=G(\lambda_{0})+(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})\,G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})+\frac{1}{2}(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})^{2}\,G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})
, where the random
\check{\lambda}
is in between
\widehat{\lambda}
and
\lambda_{0}
. We can use this identity to bound the mean of
\tilde{U}_{\mathrm{id}}
as follows:

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}[G(\widehat{\lambda})]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)|

\displaystyle=|\mathbb{E}[G(\lambda_{0})]-\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}(f,g)+\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})^{2}G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})]|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]|+\left|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})^{2}G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})]\right|\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})^{2}]\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})\}^{2}]}+\tfrac{1}{2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(\widehat{\lambda}-\lambda_{0})^{4}]\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})\}^{2}]}

\displaystyle\leq Q_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})\}^{2}]}+Q_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})\}^{2}]}

\displaystyle=Q_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\{\mathbb{E}[G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]\}^{2}+\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{Var}[G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]}+Q_{1}\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})\}^{2}]}.

(41)

Note that in the first bound we used the triangle inequality together with the fact that the U-statistic
G(\lambda_{0})
is unbiased for
\mathrm{MMD}^{2}_{r,k_{\zeta}}
, while in the third one we used Lemma
14
. Now, as for the last term, we can use the fact that
0<c\leq r(\cdot)\leq C
to show that the first and second derivatives of
\lambda\mapsto\frac{\lambda^{j}}{\{n/m+\lambda r(x)\}\{n/m+\lambda r(y)\}}
are uniformly bounded for all
\lambda>0,\,x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
j\in\{0,1,2\}
. We can thus argue as we did for the second term in (
39
) to get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\{G^{\prime\prime}(\check{\lambda})\}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq Q_{1}\max_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[k_{\zeta}^{2}(Z_{k_{1}},Z_{k_{2}})\right]\leq Q_{1}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}.

(42)

For the first two terms, observe that
G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})
is a two-sample second-order U-statistic with asymmetric kernel
\check{h}(x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2})=b_{XX}(x_{1},x_{2})k_{\zeta}(x_{1},x_{2})+b_{YY}(y_{1},y_{2})k_{\zeta}(y_{1},y_{2})+b_{XY}(x_{1},y_{2})k_{\zeta}(x_{1},y_{2})+b_{XY}(x_{2},y_{1})k_{\zeta}(x_{2},y_{1})
, where

\displaystyle\begin{cases}b_{XX}(x_{1},x_{2}):=\frac{2\lambda_{0}(n+m)^{2}r(x_{1})r(x_{2})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1})\right)\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{2})\right)}-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}m(n+m)^{2}r^{2}(x_{1})r(x_{2})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1})\right)^{2}\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{2})\right)}\\
\hskip 75.0pt-\frac{\lambda_{0}^{2}m(n+m)^{2}r(x_{1})r^{2}(x_{2})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1})\right)\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{2})\right)^{2}}\\
b_{XY}(x_{1},y_{2}):=-\frac{(n+m)^{2}\,r(x_{1})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1})\right)\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{2})\right)}+\frac{\lambda_{0}m(n+m)^{2}r^{2}(x_{1})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1}))^{2}(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{2})\right)}\\
\hskip 75.0pt+\frac{\lambda_{0}m(n+m)^{2}r(x_{1})r(y_{2})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(x_{1})\right)\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{2})\right)^{2}}\\
b_{YY}(y_{1},y_{2}):=-\frac{m(n+m)^{2}r(y_{1})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{1})\right)^{2}\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{2})\right)}-\frac{m(n+m)^{2}r(y_{2})}{\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{1})\right)\left(n+\lambda_{0}mr(y_{2})\right)^{2}}.\end{cases}

This is to say that

G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})=\frac{1}{n(n-1)m(m-1)}\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\ \sum_{j_{1}\neq j_{2}}\check{h}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}}).

We now bound the mean and the variance of
G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})
, starting from the latter. Since
\check{h}
is not symmetric in
(x_{1},x_{2})
and
(y_{1},y_{2})
, we introduce its within-sample symmetrization

\bar{h}(x_{1},x_{2},y_{1},y_{2})=\frac{1}{2!\,2!}\sum_{\pi\in{\cal S}_{2}}\sum_{\tau\in{\cal S}_{2}}\check{h}\bigl(x_{\pi(1)},x_{\pi(2)},y_{\tau(1)},y_{\tau(2)}\bigr),

where
{\cal S}_{2}
denotes the symmetric group on two elements. Because
G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})
averages
\check{h}(X_{i_{1}},X_{i_{2}},Y_{j_{1}},Y_{j_{2}})
over all ordered pairs

i_{1}\neq i_{2}
and
j_{1}\neq j_{2}
, replacing
\check{h}
by
\bar{h}
leaves the statistic unchanged.
We can thus express
G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})
as a two-sample U-statistic with a kernel symmetric within each sample, so that we may apply the classical
variance formula for two-sample U-statistics in
Lee (1990, Equation 2, pag. 38)
.
Following the simplification in
Kim et al. (2022, Equation (69))
, we obtain

\operatorname{Var}\!\bigl[G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})\bigr]\leq Q_{2}\left\{\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{10}}{n}+\frac{\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{01}}{m}+\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{m}\right)^{2}\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{22}\right\}\leq Q_{2}\left\{\frac{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{10}}{n}+\frac{\check{\sigma}^{2}_{01}}{m}+\left(\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{m}\right)^{2}\check{\sigma}^{2}_{22}\right\}

for a sufficiently large universal constant
Q_{2}>0
, where
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{10}=\operatorname{Var}_{X_{1}}[\mathbb{E}_{X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2}}\{\check{h}(X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2})\}]
,
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{01}=\operatorname{Var}_{Y_{1}}[\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},X_{2},Y_{2}}\{\check{h}(X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2})\}]
,
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{22}=\operatorname{Var}_{X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2}}[\check{h}(X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2})]
, and
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{10}
,
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{01}
,
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{22}
are defined analogously with
\bar{h}
in place of
\check{h}
. In particular, the final inequality uses that
\bar{h}
is the within-sample permutation average of
\check{h}
; therefore, by Jensen’s inequality, the corresponding projection variances for
\bar{h}
are bounded up to universal constants by those for
\check{h}
, i.e.
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{10}\lesssim\check{\sigma}^{2}_{10}
,
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{01}\lesssim\check{\sigma}^{2}_{01}
, and
\bar{\sigma}^{2}_{22}\leq\check{\sigma}^{2}_{22}
. Now, using again the boundedness assumption on
r(\cdot)
we have that
\max\{|b_{XX}(\cdot,\cdot)|,|b_{YY}(\cdot,\cdot)|,|b_{XY}(\cdot,\cdot)|\}\leq Q_{1}
, which can be used to show that
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{22}\leq Q_{1}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}
arguing as in (
42
). Furtermore,

\displaystyle\check{\sigma}^{2}_{10}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Var}_{X_{1}}[\mathbb{E}_{X_{2}}\{b_{XX}(X_{1},X_{2})k_{\zeta}(X_{1},X_{2})\}+\mathbb{E}_{Y_{2}}\{b_{XY}(X_{1},Y_{2})k_{\zeta}(X_{1},Y_{2})\}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}}[\mathbb{E}^{2}_{X_{2}}\{b_{XX}(X_{1},X_{2})k_{\zeta}(X_{1},X_{2})\}+\mathbb{E}^{2}_{Y_{2}}\{b_{XY}(X_{1},Y_{2})k_{\zeta}(X_{1},Y_{2})\}]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},X_{2}}[b^{2}_{XX}(X_{1},X_{2})k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{1},X_{2})]+2\mathbb{E}_{X_{1},Y_{2}}[b^{2}_{XY}(X_{1},Y_{2})k_{\zeta}^{2}(X_{1},Y_{2})]\leq Q_{1}M\kappa_{2}(d)\zeta^{d}.

The same holds true for
\check{\sigma}^{2}_{01}
, and shows that
n^{-1}\,\operatorname{Var}[G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]\leq Q_{1}M\kappa_{2}(d)n^{-2}\zeta^{d}
. Finally, we bound the expectation of
G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})
by
\|\varphi_{\zeta}\ast\psi_{r}\|_{2}
up to constants, where
\ast
stands for the convolution operator. Recalling
\varphi_{\zeta}(x-y)=k_{\zeta}(x,y)
and
\psi_{r}=\frac{n+m}{n+\lambda_{0}mr}(\lambda_{0}rf-g)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[G^{\prime}(\lambda_{0})]=

\displaystyle\,\mathbb{E}[b_{XX}(X_{1},X_{2})k_{\zeta}(X_{1},X_{2})]+\mathbb{E}[b_{YY}(Y_{1},Y_{2})k_{\zeta}(Y_{1},Y_{2})]+2\mathbb{E}[b_{XY}(X,Y)k_{\zeta}(X,Y)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2m(n+m)^{2}\lambda_{0}^{2}r^{2}(X_{1})r(X_{2})\varphi_{\zeta}(X_{1}-X_{2})}{(n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{1}))^{2}(n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{2}))}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2(n+m)^{2}\lambda_{0}r(X_{1})r(X_{2})\varphi_{\zeta}(X_{1}-X_{2})}{(n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{1}))(n+\lambda_{0}mr(X_{2}))}\right]
